[2026-10-13T11:16:48.890Z] [INFO]           "command": "cat > /tmp/pr.md <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\nRefs #176. Fixes #174 (JavaScript). #175 (Cypress) is **not implemented yet**; see \"Remaining work\" below.\n\n### #174: an embedder can own Ctrl+C and hand the browser off\n**Requirements and what this PR does for each:**\n- **A launch-level option, managed by default.** `interruption: 'managed' | 'caller'` is accepted by `launchBrowser`, `launchRealBrowser`, `launchWebDriver` and `startProcess`. Any other value throws a `TypeError`.\n- **What `'caller'` does.** It releases the child from command-stream's global SIGINT forwarder. Engine launches get `handleSIGINT: false`, and the ChromeDriver started by the Selenium real launch follows the browser's mode.\n- **Detach while the exact browser and tab stay open.** `session.detach()` and `session.handoff()` return a JSON hand-off: `version`, `engine`, `cdpEndpoint`, `webSocketDebuggerUrl`, `targetId`, `pid`, `userDataDir`, `temporaryProfile`, …\n- **Reconnect without recreating state.** `reconnectBrowser(handoff)` never launches a browser and never creates or navigates a page. It checks the browser's identity through `/json/version` and requires the exact tab. If either check fails, it throws `BrowserHandoffError`.\n- **Explicit close.** `close()` closes the owned browser, waits for the real process to exit (escalating to SIGKILL), and removes a temporary profile.\n\n**Root cause:** command-stream 2.0.2 installs a process-wide SIGINT handler. It forwards SIGINT to the process group of every child that is still running, before the embedder's own handlers run. Playwright and Puppeteer also close the browser on SIGINT by default. `experiments/issue-174/` reproduces this.\n\n**Tests:**\n- Unit: `js/tests/unit/utilities/interruption.test.js` and `js/tests/unit/browser/browser-handoff.test.js` (using a fake CDP endpoint), plus a `handleSIGINT` test in `launcher.test.js`.\n- E2E against real Chrome: `js/tests/e2e/browser-handoff.e2e.test.js`, added to `parity.yml`.\n  - Managed mode: Chrome dies on Ctrl+C.\n  - Caller mode, for Playwright, Puppeteer and Selenium: the supervisor exits with 130, Chrome stays alive, reconnecting finds the same tab title, and `close()` reaps the pid and removes the profile.\n\n**Docs:** `docs/embedding-and-handoff.md`, `examples/browser-handoff.mjs` and the README API entries, plus a minor changeset.\n\n## Remaining work (not in this PR yet)\n- **Python and Rust ports of #174.** They are recorded as a documented gap in `docs/feature-parity/limitations.json`. A hand-off is plain JSON with a CDP endpoint and target id, so those languages can attach today with `connect_browser`.\n- **#175 Cypress.** The planned design, based on research:\n  - a `browser-commander/cypress` plugin that captures the browser's `webSocketDebuggerUrl` through Cypress's `after:browser:launch`, the same approach `@cypress/puppeteer` uses;\n  - `cy.task` handlers that attach Playwright (`connectOverCDP`), Puppeteer, or Selenium (`debuggerAddress`) and always detach. Each handler returns `null` instead of `undefined`, because `cy.task` requires that.\n  - Chromium-family browsers and Electron only. Firefox uses WebDriver BiDi, so it needs an explicit error.\nEOF\ngh pr edit 177 --title \"feat: caller-owned Ctrl+C and browser hand-off (#174)\" --body-file /tmp/pr.md >/dev/null && echo ok",

[2026-10-13T11:32:27.352Z] [INFO]         "text": "Issue #174 is done for JavaScript in PR #177, and CI now passes on every check. Issue #175 (Cypress) isn't started, and the Python and Rust versions of #174 aren't done, so I've left the PR as a draft.\n\n**What #174 needed and what was built:**\n- **Ctrl+C belongs to the caller.** `interruption: 'caller'` works on `launchBrowser`, `launchRealBrowser`, `launchWebDriver` and `startProcess`. The default stays `'managed'`.\n  - The cause was a SIGINT handler in the command-stream library that forwards Ctrl+C to every child process before the application's own handler runs. On top of that, Playwright and Puppeteer close the browser on Ctrl+C by default.\n  - Caller mode stops that forwarding and turns off the engines' own Ctrl+C handling. The ChromeDriver started for Selenium follows the same setting.\n- **Detach and reconnect to the exact tab.**\n  - `session.detach()` leaves the browser running and returns a small JSON description of the exact browser and tab.\n  - `reconnectBrowser(handoff)` attaches to that same tab without launching a browser or navigating. If the browser was replaced or the tab is gone, it throws `BrowserHandoffError`.\n- **Closing really closes.** `close()` waits until the browser process has exited (force-killing it if needed) and deletes a temporary profile.\n\n**Testing:**\n- The JS unit suite passes (1888 tests). Three of the new tests rely on Unix signals, so they skip on Windows; that was the cause of the last Windows CI failure.\n- Against a real Chrome, Ctrl+C kills the browser in the default mode. In caller mode, Playwright, Puppeteer and Selenium all reconnect to the same tab, and `close()` ends the process and deletes the profile.\n- I also ran `examples/browser-handoff.mjs` end to end.\n- Docs are in `docs/embedding-and-handoff.md` and the READMEs, with a minor-version changeset.\n\n**Not done yet:**\n- **Python and Rust versions of #174.** I started background agents to port it, but both stopped on an API rate limit before changing any files. I recorded this as a known gap in `docs/feature-parity/limitations.json` so the parity check passes. The hand-off is plain JSON, so Python and Rust can already attach to a handed-off browser with `connect_browser`.\n- **#175 Cypress.** I removed the Cypress entry from the parity list until it exists. The planned design is in the PR description:\n  - a `browser-commander/cypress` plugin that picks up the browser's debugging address when Cypress launches it, as `@cypress/puppeteer` does;\n  - `cy.task` handlers that attach Playwright, Puppeteer or Selenium to that browser;\n  - Chrome-family browsers and Electron only, with a clear error for Firefox.\n\nBecause of these gaps, the PR description says \"Fixes #174\" (JS) and only \"Refs #176\"; it doesn't close #175.\n\nI also renamed the PR to \"feat: caller-owned Ctrl+C and browser hand-off (#174)\". Before running, an automated commit had added a Cypress entry to the parity list, which was failing CI. A broken link to the new guide failed CI as well. I fixed both."

[2026-10-13T11:32:27.422Z] [INFO]   "result": "Issue #174 is done for JavaScript in PR #177, and CI now passes on every check. Issue #175 (Cypress) isn't started, and the Python and Rust versions of #174 aren't done, so I've left the PR as a draft.\n\n**What #174 needed and what was built:**\n- **Ctrl+C belongs to the caller.** `interruption: 'caller'` works on `launchBrowser`, `launchRealBrowser`, `launchWebDriver` and `startProcess`. The default stays `'managed'`.\n  - The cause was a SIGINT handler in the command-stream library that forwards Ctrl+C to every child process before the application's own handler runs. On top of that, Playwright and Puppeteer close the browser on Ctrl+C by default.\n  - Caller mode stops that forwarding and turns off the engines' own Ctrl+C handling. The ChromeDriver started for Selenium follows the same setting.\n- **Detach and reconnect to the exact tab.**\n  - `session.detach()` leaves the browser running and returns a small JSON description of the exact browser and tab.\n  - `reconnectBrowser(handoff)` attaches to that same tab without launching a browser or navigating. If the browser was replaced or the tab is gone, it throws `BrowserHandoffError`.\n- **Closing really closes.** `close()` waits until the browser process has exited (force-killing it if needed) and deletes a temporary profile.\n\n**Testing:**\n- The JS unit suite passes (1888 tests). Three of the new tests rely on Unix signals, so they skip on Windows; that was the cause of the last Windows CI failure.\n- Against a real Chrome, Ctrl+C kills the browser in the default mode. In caller mode, Playwright, Puppeteer and Selenium all reconnect to the same tab, and `close()` ends the process and deletes the profile.\n- I also ran `examples/browser-handoff.mjs` end to end.\n- Docs are in `docs/embedding-and-handoff.md` and the READMEs, with a minor-version changeset.\n\n**Not done yet:**\n- **Python and Rust versions of #174.** I started background agents to port it, but both stopped on an API rate limit before changing any files. I recorded this as a known gap in `docs/feature-parity/limitations.json` so the parity check passes. The hand-off is plain JSON, so Python and Rust can already attach to a handed-off browser with `connect_browser`.\n- **#175 Cypress.** I removed the Cypress entry from the parity list until it exists. The planned design is in the PR description:\n  - a `browser-commander/cypress` plugin that picks up the browser's debugging address when Cypress launches it, as `@cypress/puppeteer` does;\n  - `cy.task` handlers that attach Playwright, Puppeteer or Selenium to that browser;\n  - Chrome-family browsers and Electron only, with a clear error for Firefox.\n\nBecause of these gaps, the PR description says \"Fixes #174\" (JS) and only \"Refs #176\"; it doesn't close #175.\n\nI also renamed the PR to \"feat: caller-owned Ctrl+C and browser hand-off (#174)\". Before running, an automated commit had added a Cypress entry to the parity list, which was failing CI. A broken link to the new guide failed CI as well. I fixed both.",

embedding supervisor needs to own Ctrl+C cancellation while preserving an already detected CAPTCHA page for a person. Ordinary `launchBrowser()` uses the managed command-stream process runner. Its SIGINT handler forwards SIGINT to Chrome before an embedding handler can decide to detach and preserve the browser.\n\nObserved in isolated native Chrome fixtures with browser-commander0.31.1 + command-stream2.0.2: SIGKILL supervisor recovery retains the same Chrome PID/target; a pending-page SIGINT supervisor exit can kill Chrome despite our own handler choosing to detach. No real CAPTCHA was solved or bypassed. The source paths remain in 0.31.2 (`spawnWithStartProcess` in `src/browser/real-browser.js`; SIGINT forwarding in command-stream's runner state).\n\nPlease expose a documented scoped launch/lifetime option, or a public managed-process detach operation, that lets an embedding supervisor own interruption without removing launch-installed process handlers. Ordinary managed launch cancellation should remain the default. Required behavior for a persistent embedding: detach/stop the controller while the exact original browser/tab remains, then reconnect it; never launch or navigate to recreate a human-waiting state. Explicit owned-browser close must still close and await the actual child.\n\n`connectOrLaunch()` already provides detached-process machinery, but its autonomous heartbeat/watchdog lifetime differs from our existing supervisor's pause-aware idle/lease policy. A reusable launch/process option would avoid a second lifetime controller and let consumers implement their own human handoff gates cleanly.\n\nConsumer workaround in [lazada-search PR41](https://github.com/konard/lazada-search/pull/41): only the dedicated browser supervisor snapshots existing SIGINT handlers before launch and removes handlers installed by that launch, then owns normal shutdown versus pending-challenge detach. It does not change the user's or calling application's handlers. Exact-target native regression coverage and original failures are being committed under `docs/acceptance/challenge-window-retention/`; consumer issue is [lazada-search42](https://github.com/konard/lazada-search/issues/42).\n\nExisting reports were checked: no SIGINT report found, and persistent issues140,150,151,171 do not describe this scoped interruption-ownership API. This is an embedding API feature request, not a request to change ordinary launch cancellation embedding supervisor needs to own Ctrl+C cancellation while preserving an already detected CAPTCHA page for a person. Ordinary `launchBrowser()` uses the managed command-stream process runner. Its SIGINT handler forwards SIGINT to Chrome before an embedding handler can decide to detach and preserve the browser.\n\nObserved in isolated native Chrome fixtures with browser-commander0.31.1 + command-stream2.0.2: SIGKILL supervisor recovery retains the same Chrome PID/target; a pending-page SIGINT supervisor exit can kill Chrome despite our own handler choosing to detach. No real CAPTCHA was solved or bypassed. The source paths remain in 0.31.2 (`spawnWithStartProcess` in `src/browser/real-browser.js`; SIGINT forwarding in command-stream's runner state).\n\nPlease expose a documented scoped launch/lifetime option, or a public managed-process detach operation, that lets an embedding supervisor own interruption without removing launch-installed process handlers. Ordinary managed launch cancellation should remain the default. Required behavior for a persistent embedding: detach/stop the controller while the exact original browser/tab remains, then reconnect it; never launch or navigate to recreate a human-waiting state. Explicit owned-browser close must still close and await the actual child.\n\n`connectOrLaunch()` already provides detached-process machinery, but its autonomous heartbeat/watchdog lifetime differs from our existing supervisor's pause-aware idle/lease policy. A reusable launch/process option would avoid a second lifetime controller and let consumers implement their own human handoff gates cleanly.\n\nConsumer workaround in [lazada-search PR41](https://github.com/konard/lazada-search/pull/41): only the dedicated browser supervisor snapshots existing SIGINT handlers before launch and removes handlers installed by that launch, then owns normal shutdown versus pending-challenge detach. It does not change the user's or calling application's handlers. Exact-target native regression coverage and original failures are being committed under `docs/acceptance/challenge-window-retention/`; consumer issue is [lazada-search42](https://github.com/konard/lazada-search/issues/42).\n\nExisting reports were checked: no SIGINT report found, and persistent issues140,150,151,171 do not describe this scoped interruption-ownership API. This is an embedding API feature request, not a request to change ordinary launch cancellation Summary\n\nRefs #176. Fixes #174 (JavaScript). #175 (Cypress) is **not implemented yet**; see \"Remaining work\" below.\n\n### #174: an embedder can own Ctrl+C and hand the browser off\n**Requirements and what this PR does for each:**\n- **A launch-level option, managed by default.** `interruption: 'managed' | 'caller'` is accepted by `launchBrowser`, `launchRealBrowser`, `launchWebDriver` and `startProcess`. Any other value throws a `TypeError`.\n- **What `'caller'` does.** It releases the child from command-stream's global SIGINT forwarder. Engine launches get `handleSIGINT: false`, and the ChromeDriver started by the Selenium real launch follows the browser's mode.\n- **Detach while the exact browser and tab stay open.** `session.detach()` and `session.handoff()` return a JSON hand-off: `version`, `engine`, `cdpEndpoint`, `webSocketDebuggerUrl`, `targetId`, `pid`, `userDataDir`, `temporaryProfile`, …\n- **Reconnect without recreating state.** `reconnectBrowser(handoff)` never launches a browser and never creates or navigates a page. It checks the browser's identity through `/json/version` and requires the exact tab. If either check fails, it throws `BrowserHandoffError`.\n- **Explicit close.** `close()` closes the owned browser, waits for the real process to exit (escalating to SIGKILL), and removes a temporary profile.\n\n**Root cause:** command-stream 2.0.2 installs a process-wide SIGINT handler. It forwards SIGINT to the process group of every child that is still running, before the embedder's own handlers run. Playwright and Puppeteer also close the browser on SIGINT by default. `experiments/issue-174/` reproduces this.\n\n**Tests:**\n- Unit: `js/tests/unit/utilities/interruption.test.js` and `js/tests/unit/browser/browser-handoff.test.js` (using a fake CDP endpoint), plus a `handleSIGINT` test in `launcher.test.js`.\n- E2E against real Chrome: `js/tests/e2e/browser-handoff.e2e.test.js`, added to `parity.yml`.\n  - Managed mode: Chrome dies on Ctrl+C.\n  - Caller mode, for Playwright, Puppeteer and Selenium: the supervisor exits with 130, Chrome stays alive, reconnecting finds the same tab title, and `close()` reaps the pid and removes the profile.\n\n**Docs:** `docs/embedding-and-handoff.md`, `examples/browser-handoff.mjs` and the README API entries, plus a minor changeset.\n\n## Remaining work (not in this PR yet)\n- **Python and Rust ports of #174.** They are recorded as a documented gap in `docs/feature-parity/limitations.json`. A hand-off is plain JSON with a CDP endpoint and target id, so those languages can attach today with `connect_browser`.\n- **#175 Cypress.** The planned design, based on research:\n  - a `browser-commander/cypress` plugin that captures the browser's `webSocketDebuggerUrl` through Cypress's `after:browser:launch`, the same approach `@cypress/puppeteer` uses;\n  - `cy.task` handlers that attach Playwright (`connectOverCDP`), Puppeteer, or Selenium (`debuggerAddress`) and always detach. Each handler returns `null` instead of `undefined`, because `cy.task` requires that.\n  - Chromium-family browsers and Electron only. Firefox uses WebDriver BiDi, so it needs an explicit error.\n","created_at":"2026-10-13T07:10:48Z","updated_at":"2026-10-13T11:32:38Z","closed_at":null,"merged_at":null,"merge_commit_sha":"284f5ecaccf2b9e24c89df6effa714e885d77f12","assignees":[{"login":"konard","id":1431904,"node_id":"MDQ6VXNlcjE0MzE5MDQ=","avatar_url":"https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/u/1431904?v=4","gravatar_id":"","url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard","html_url":"https://github.com/konard","followers_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/followers","following_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/following{/other_user}","gists_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/gists{/gist_id}","starred_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/starred{/owner}{/repo}","subscriptions_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/subscriptions","organizations_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/orgs","repos_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/repos","events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/events{/privacy}","received_events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/konard/received_events","type":"User","user_view_type":"public","site_admin":false}],"requested_reviewers":[],"requested_teams":[],"labels":[],"milestone":null,"draft":true,"commits_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/link-foundation/browser-commander/pulls/177/commits","review_comments_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/link-foundation/browser-commander/pulls/177/comments","review_comment_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/link-foundation/browser-commander/pulls/comments{/number}","comments_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/link-foundation/browser-commander/issues/177/comments","statuses_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/link-foundation/browser-commander/statuses/fb597c78bd61bb7299e4d14daa7834aaf2fd7048","head":{"label":"link-foundation:issue-176-9aa2b52b0165","ref":"issue-176-9aa2b52b0165","sha":"fb597c78bd61bb7299e4d14daa7834aaf2fd7048","user":{"login":"link-foundation","id":176174013,"node_id":"O_kgDOCoAzvQ","avatar_url":"https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/u/1761
